FILE: README.md

# GridSense - Team 3

EV charging and load-management demonstration using simulated sensors, MQTT,
Node-RED, SQLite, five ML models and actuator feedback.

For the configured **public internet MQTT broker**, follow [CLOUD_MQTT.md](CLOUD_MQTT.md).
On the project laptop run `./start.ps1 -PublicBroker`; on the sensor laptop run
`python simulator.py --public-broker`. Copy the four sensor Python files **plus
`mqtt-public.json`** to the sensor laptop. This uses `broker.emqx.io:8883` with
verified TLS and project-specific topics. No account or password is required.
The LAN setup below remains available as an alternative.

## Architecture

The implementation follows the branching diagram in `refererncesss.png`:

```text
Sensor laptop: Python sensor/plant simulator
    -> ev/telemetry -> Project laptop: Aedes MQTT broker -> Node-RED
        -> live dashboard
        -> SQLite commit -> database read -> ML/evaluation -> decision
            -> ev/actuator -> same MQTT broker -> sensor laptop actuator
                -> ev/ack -> MQTT -> Node-RED -> SQLite control metrics
```

The live dashboard and database are parallel outputs. Analytics reads committed
records and does not depend on an open browser. The actuator runs inside the sensor
process; do not run `actuator.py` separately. Run only one live simulator per station.

## Install on the project laptop

Install Python 3.10+ and Node.js 20 or later. In the extracted `GridSense` folder:

```powershell
python -m venv .venv
./.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pip install -r requirements-station.txt
npm.cmd ci --prefix node-red
```

`requirements-station.txt` uses compatible version ranges, including Python 3.11.
Run `python prepare_demo.py` once after installing dependencies to train a model
bundle compatible with that environment. This installation step stages real-or-
fallback history in a temporary SQLite database. The demo ZIP supplies
`setup_demo.ps1` to automate installation and preparation.

## Two-laptop demonstration

Both laptops must be on a trusted Wi-Fi/LAN that permits connections between devices.
Guest Wi-Fi may block this. On the Windows **project laptop**:

1. Run `ipconfig` and note the connected adapter's IPv4 address.
2. Stop any previous GridSense launcher, then run `./start.ps1 -RemoteSensor`.
3. Allow TCP 1883 through Windows Firewall on the private network. If needed, run
   the following once in Administrator PowerShell:

```powershell
New-NetFirewallRule -DisplayName "GridSense MQTT LAN" -Direction Inbound -Action Allow -Protocol TCP -LocalPort 1883 -Profile Private -RemoteAddress LocalSubnet
```

The launcher starts Aedes on all IPv4 interfaces and skips the local simulator.
An already-running broker must separately accept LAN connections. The demo broker
has no authentication/TLS; do not expose it to the internet.

On the **sensor laptop**, copy `simulator.py`, `actuator.py`, `station_core.py` and
`tariff.py` into one folder, then run:

```bash
python -m pip install "paho-mqtt>=2.1,<3"
python simulator.py --broker-host 192.168.1.10
```

Replace the example IP with the project laptop's address. Use `python3` where needed.
Add `--tou-demo` for the feasible evening tariff demonstration. The sensor laptop
needs neither Node-RED nor ML dependencies. Keep both laptop clocks synchronized
for meaningful sensor-to-actuator latency measurements.

Open on the project laptop:

- Dashboard: `http://127.0.0.1:1880/dashboard/station`
- Flow editor: `http://127.0.0.1:1880`

Changing dashboard readings demonstrate incoming telemetry. Accepted `ev/ack`
messages in Node-RED's debug sidebar demonstrate the return control path. A Windows
sensor laptop can check reachability with `Test-NetConnection 192.168.1.10 -Port 1883`.
Press Ctrl+C in each launcher/simulator terminal to stop.

## Single-laptop demonstration

On Windows run `./start.ps1` or `./start.ps1 -TouDemo`.
On Linux/macOS activate the virtual environment, start
`node node-red/demo-broker.cjs` in one terminal, and run `bash start.sh` in another.
The shell launcher is for a single laptop. For a Linux/macOS project host with a
remote sensor, run `node node-red/demo-broker.cjs --lan`, `python station_service.py`
and `npm --prefix node-red start` in separate terminals; do not run a local simulator.

## Train or refresh models

The demo setup trains locally compatible models; source evaluation reports remain outside the demo ZIP. SQLite starts
empty on a fresh extraction; the application creates it automatically. To reproduce
training, keep the project services running and use another terminal:

```powershell
./.venv/Scripts/python.exe simulator.py --history
Invoke-RestMethod http://127.0.0.1:8765/health
# Wait until history_rows is 1008, then:
./.venv/Scripts/python.exe ml_train.py
```

History replay can also run on the sensor laptop with `--broker-host <project-IP>`.
Restart the project services after training; model loading occurs once at startup.
If no model bundle exists, decisions explicitly report `untrained_safe_rules`.
Only load trusted model files. Training uses 28 days, validation seven days and
testing seven days of synthetic hourly observations stored in SQLite.

## Verification

```powershell
./.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v
node node-red/validate-upgrades.cjs
```

For the full local MQTT integration check, first stop both laptops' demo processes
and free ports 1880/1883/8765, then run:

```powershell
./.venv/Scripts/python.exe tests/live_check.py
```

This starts a test stack, writes test rows into `station.db`, retrains models,
verifies storage and actuator feedback, refreshes `artifacts/live_check.json`,
and stops its processes. It does not test connectivity between physical laptops.
Offline comparisons: `python evaluate_control.py` and `python evaluate_tou.py`.

## Project files and submission

| File or directory | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `simulator.py`, `actuator.py`, `station_core.py` | Sensor/plant simulation and command application |
| `station_store.py`, `station_service.py` | Committed SQLite storage and local HTTP adapter |
| `ml_train.py`, `decision_engine.py` | Database-trained models and evaluated decisions |
| `tariff.py`, `tou_scheduler.py` | Simulated INR tariffs and constrained charging schedules |
| `node-red/` | MQTT flows, dashboard templates, broker and presentation builder |
| `tests/` | Behavior, architecture and integration checks |
| `artifacts/` | Trained models and measured evaluation reports |
| `GridSense_Final_Report.md` | Current methods, results and limitations |
| `GridSense_Presentation.pptx` | Presentation slides |

Rebuild slides with `node node-red/build-presentation.cjs`. Build the upload with
`python package_submission.py`. Upload **`submission/GridSense_Team3.zip` only**.
It contains source, dependency manifests, tests, documentation, models, metrics,
slides and SHA-256 checksums. It excludes local databases, installed dependencies,
logs, Git history and the local cleanup backup.

ACN session behavior is preferred; SoC, tariffs and actuation remain simulated.
Real hardware and real-world prediction accuracy remain unverified.

## Real data source (ACN-Data)

ACN-Data is the preferred source for historical charging behavior and replay fleets.
Register and retrieve an API token at [Caltech ACN-Data](https://ev.caltech.edu/dataset).
On the **project laptop only**, set `ACN_DATA_TOKEN` in the current terminal (enter
its value locally, never commit it or paste it into chat):

```powershell
$env:ACN_DATA_TOKEN = '<your API token>'
# With the project services already running:
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe simulator.py --public-broker --history
Invoke-RestMethod http://127.0.0.1:8765/health
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe ml_train.py
```

Restart the project after training. `/health` exposes `data_source`, `dataset`,
`history_rows` and `active_history_rows`. The model report and dashboard also show
`data_source`. `ml_train.py --dataset acn-caltech-v1` explicitly selects real data;
otherwise training selects the most recently ingested dataset without mixing sources.

The adapter uses `acnportal.acndata.DataClient(token).get_sessions(...)` from the
published 0.3.3 release (`acnportal>=0.3.3,<0.4`). Session queries use bounded date
windows and time series. It discovers the latest available date rather than assuming
that data extends to today; optionally set `ACN_DATA_END=2020-02-12` to select a
specific exclusive end date (site-local midnight). The default site is Caltech;
`acn_data.fetch_sessions` also accepts `jpl` and `office001` for explicit use.

Fetched sessions are cached locally in `artifacts/acn_cache.json` for 24 hours,
keyed by site/start/end. Calls have HTTP timeouts and a bounded 300-second/5,000-session
fetch budget; incomplete downloads are rejected instead of reported as complete data.
The most recent incomplete site-local day is excluded. At least 18 days with observed
sessions are required; missing arrival hours within the fetched interval are zeros.
Each session's total delivered energy is assigned to its arrival-hour bin: this is
arrival-associated energy demand, **not** the measured power consumed in that hour.

If the token is missing, access fails, or data is insufficient, `[FALLBACK] Using
synthetic history` is logged. The unchanged deterministic generator is used and
reported as `synthetic-fallback`; prediction validity is limited to the simulation.
The service logs the fallback when that history reaches SQLite. To force offline data:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe simulator.py --public-broker --history --synthetic-only
```

A successful project-laptop history replay also prepares `sender/acn_vehicles.json`.
Copy that file alongside the five sender files to **your sensor laptop**, then run
`python simulator.py --public-broker`. This file contains at most six derived vehicle
records from the latest session day, not raw current traces or credentials. The sensor
still installs **only paho-mqtt**. ACNPortal and requests remain project-laptop
requirements. If no prepared fleet is available, the standalone sensor explicitly
falls back. `--synthetic-only` bypasses even the prepared fleet. `--tou-demo` remains
the deliberately synthetic tariff scenario.

ACN-Data normally does not contain measured SoC or a vehicle make/model/year.
SoC is model-derived from native current timestamps, recorded session energy and
an assumed/known capacity. OpenEV's documented API requires hosting; the adapter
uses the project's public JSON release (discovered through its GitHub releases API)
when vehicle identity is supplied. Unknown/unavailable/ambiguous capacity uses a
logged **40 kWh assumption**, not an assertion about the actual car. Lookups are
cached in memory. See [OpenEV data consumption](https://open-ev-data.github.io/latest/dataset/HOW_TO_CONSUME/).

Current, voltage and efficiency determine trajectory shape; the trace is normalized
to `kWhDelivered`, so the energy-to-SoC convention matches the requested model.
Requested energy is preferred for initial-SoC inference; observed taper permits an
explicit 95%-final-SoC assumption, otherwise the supplied/default 30% initial SoC is
used. SoC is bounded to [0,1], with excess unrepresentable energy reported separately.
This is a calibrated simulation, not measured battery energy or a BMS model. Current
power and real SoC are not identified from session energy alone. Overnight departures
retain their duration; live actuation uses the existing piecewise taper model.

Tokens are read from the environment (or an explicit in-memory API argument), never
logged or written to cache. The submission ZIP excludes `.env*`, downloaded sessions,
ACN caches, and prepared fleets. Only aggregate energy/derived fleet data crosses
MQTT; do not add personal data to public broker messages.

## Saved ACN downloads and offline reuse

Download incrementally on the project laptop after setting ACN_DATA_TOKEN:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe acn_data.py --download --seconds 120
```

Sessions are saved after every response in `data/acn_downloads/caltech-YYYY-MM-DD.json`.
Run the same command again to resume incomplete pages and skip completed days. The
seconds limit is checked between requests; an in-flight request can take additional
time. Each file records whether the whole day was downloaded. Credentials and user
identity fields are excluded. These durable historical snapshots do not expire like
the separate 24-hour API cache; rerunning the downloader extends the saved snapshot.

The root simulator automatically prefers this folder, with no token or network
needed to read it. Live mode can use valid sessions from a partial day. History mode
requires at least 18 observed days within a contiguous, completely downloaded
interval. Partial or missing days are never converted to zero-demand training rows.
Insufficient saved history logs a warning and uses synthetic fallback immediately.
A live ACN fleet therefore does not imply the ML training data is also ACN; check the
model report and /health separately. `--synthetic-only` bypasses saved data too.

The downloader also writes `sender/acn_vehicles.json` when usable vehicles exist.
Include that file when manually sending the sender folder. The standalone sender
can replay it with only paho-mqtt installed. Raw downloaded sessions remain on the
project laptop and are excluded from Git and the submission ZIP. The ZIP includes
the downloader code; download there or copy the data folder separately if needed.



================================================
FILE: acn_data.py
================================================
"""ACN-Data access, bounded caching, and explicitly model-derived battery SoC.

ACNPortal's published API is DataClient(token).get_sessions(...), not a module
function. Battery specifications are optional; ACN does not normally identify cars.
"""
import hashlib
import json
import logging
import math
import os
import threading
import time
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from email.utils import parsedate_to_datetime, format_datetime
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from types import SimpleNamespace
from unittest.mock import patch
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo

try:
    import requests
except ImportError:
    requests = None
try:
    from acnportal.acndata import DataClient
except ImportError:
    DataClient = None

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)
CACHE_PATH = Path(__file__).with_name('artifacts')/'acn_cache.json'
CACHE_TTL = 24*60*60
LOCAL_DIR = Path(__file__).with_name('data')/'acn_downloads'
API = 'https://ev.caltech.edu/api/v1/'
_client_lock = threading.Lock()


class AcnDataError(RuntimeError):
    pass


class AcnDataNotConfigured(AcnDataError):
    pass


def _token(value=None):
    value = value if value is not None else os.environ.get('ACN_DATA_TOKEN')
    if not value:
        raise AcnDataNotConfigured('ACN_DATA_TOKEN is not configured')
    return value


def _datetime(value):
    if isinstance(value, datetime):
        result = value
    else:
        try:
            result = datetime.fromisoformat(str(value).replace('Z', '+00:00'))
        except ValueError:
            result = parsedate_to_datetime(str(value))
    return result.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc) if result.tzinfo is None else result


def _local(session, field):
    return _datetime(session[field]).astimezone(ZoneInfo(session.get('timezone', 'America/Los_Angeles')))


def _read_cache():
    try:
        data = json.loads(CACHE_PATH.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
        return data if isinstance(data, dict) else {}
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return {}


def _cache_get(key):
    record = _read_cache().get(key, {})
    age = time.time()-record.get('fetched_at', 0)
    return record.get('data') if 0 <= age < CACHE_TTL else None


def _cache_put(key, data):
    records = {k: v for k, v in _read_cache().items()
               if 0 <= time.time()-v.get('fetched_at', 0) < CACHE_TTL}
    records[key] = {'fetched_at': time.time(), 'data': data}
    try:
        CACHE_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        temporary = CACHE_PATH.with_suffix('.tmp')
        temporary.write_text(json.dumps(records, default=lambda x: x.isoformat(), allow_nan=False), encoding='utf-8')
        temporary.replace(CACHE_PATH)
    except OSError:
        LOG.warning('ACN cache could not be written; continuing without disk cache')


def _get(url, **kwargs):
    if requests is None:
        raise AcnDataNotConfigured('Install project requirements to enable ACN-Data access')
    response = requests.get(url, timeout=(5, 20), **kwargs)
    response.raise_for_status()
    return response


def _current_values(session):
    series = session.get('chargingCurrent') or {}
    values = series.get('current', []) if isinstance(series, dict) else series
    if values and isinstance(values[0], dict):
        values = [point.get('current', point.get('value', 0)) for point in values]
    return [max(0.0, float(value)) for value in values if math.isfinite(float(value))]


def _clean_session(session):
    cleaned = {k: session[k] for k in ('sessionID', 'connectionTime', 'disconnectTime',
               'kWhDelivered', 'chargingCurrent', 'timezone', 'make', 'model', 'year') if k in session}
    cleaned['userInputs'] = [{'kWhRequested': r['kWhRequested']} for r in
                             (session.get('userInputs') or []) if 'kWhRequested' in r]
    return cleaned


def _write_json(path, value):
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    temporary = path.with_suffix('.tmp')
    temporary.write_text(json.dumps(value, default=lambda x: x.isoformat(), allow_nan=False), encoding='utf-8')
    temporary.replace(path)


def saved_sessions(for_history=False, days=42):
    """Read durable downloads without a token. Partial days are live-demo only."""
    records = []
    for path in sorted(LOCAL_DIR.glob('caltech-*.json')):
        try:
            record = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
            if record.get('site') != 'caltech':
                continue
            day = datetime.strptime(record['day'], '%Y-%m-%d').date()
            valid = []
            for session in record['sessions']:
                if _local(session, 'connectionTime').date() != day:
                    raise ValueError('Session outside saved day')
                energy = float(session['kWhDelivered'])
                if math.isfinite(energy) and energy >= .5 and max(_current_values(session), default=0) > 0:
                    valid.append(session)
            records.append((day, record.get('complete') is True, valid))
        except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):
            LOG.warning('Ignoring invalid saved ACN day: %s', path.name)
    if not for_history:
        return [s for _, _, sessions in records[-days:] for s in sessions]
    # Do not turn undownloaded days or partial pages into zero demand.
    runs, run = [], []
    for record in records:
        day, complete, _ = record
        if not complete or (run and day-run[-1][0] != timedelta(days=1)):
            if run:
                runs.append(run)
            run = []
        if complete:
            run.append(record)
    if run:
        runs.append(run)
    for run in reversed(runs):
        selected = run[-days:]
        if sum(bool(r[2]) for r in selected) >= 18:
            LOG.warning('Using saved ACN history: %d completely downloaded days', len(selected))
            return [s for _, _, sessions in selected for s in sessions]
    LOG.warning('Saved ACN history is insufficient: need 18 observed days in a completely downloaded interval')
    raise AcnDataError('Saved history needs 18 observed days in a completely downloaded interval')


def download_local(days=42, seconds=120):
    """Resume per-day pages, saving every received session; keep partial progress."""
    credential = _token()
    start, end = recent_window(days)
    deadline = time.monotonic()+seconds
    LOCAL_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    day = start
    while day < end and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        path = LOCAL_DIR/('caltech-'+day.date().isoformat()+'.json')
        record = {'site': 'caltech', 'day': day.date().isoformat(), 'complete': False,
                  'next_page': 1, 'sessions': []}
        if path.exists():
            record = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
        if record['complete']:
            day += timedelta(days=1)
            continue
        until = day+timedelta(days=1)
        cond = f'connectionTime >= "{format_datetime(day.astimezone(timezone.utc), usegmt=True)}" and connectionTime < "{format_datetime(until.astimezone(timezone.utc), usegmt=True)}"'
        try:
            while time.monotonic() < deadline:
                response = _get(API+'sessions/caltech/ts/', auth=(credential, ''),
                                params={'where': cond, 'sort': 'connectionTime', 'max_results': 1,
                                        'page': record['next_page']})
                payload = response.json()
                items = payload['_items']
                if not isinstance(items, list) or '_links' not in payload:
                    raise AcnDataError('Invalid paginated response')
                known = {s['sessionID'] for s in record['sessions']}
                for item in items:
                    clean = _clean_session(item)
                    if clean['sessionID'] not in known:
                        record['sessions'].append(clean)
                        known.add(clean['sessionID'])
                        _write_json(path, record)
                record['next_page'] += 1
                record['complete'] = 'next' not in payload['_links']
                _write_json(path, record)
                LOG.warning('Saved ACN %s: %d sessions (%s)', record['day'], len(record['sessions']),
                            'complete day' if record['complete'] else 'partial day')
                if record['complete']:
                    break
        except Exception as exc:
            LOG.warning('ACN download paused (%s); saved progress retained', type(exc).__name__)
            break
        day = until
    sessions = saved_sessions()
    LOG.warning('Saved usable ACN sessions: %d; folder: %s', len(sessions), LOCAL_DIR)
    return sessions


def fetch_sessions(site='caltech', start=None, end=None, min_kwh=0.5, token=None):
    """Return filtered time-series sessions for an explicitly bounded UTC window."""
    credential = _token(token)  # Never cache credentials, authentication headers or exception text.
    if site not in ('caltech', 'jpl', 'office001'):
        raise ValueError('Unsupported ACN site')
    if start is None or end is None:
        raise ValueError('start and end are required')
    start, end = _datetime(start).astimezone(timezone.utc), _datetime(end).astimezone(timezone.utc)
    if end <= start or end-start > timedelta(days=62):
        raise ValueError('ACN queries must cover more than zero and at most 62 days')
    if not math.isfinite(min_kwh) or min_kwh < 0:
        raise ValueError('min_kwh must be nonnegative')
    key = json.dumps([site, start.isoformat(), end.isoformat()])
    sessions = _cache_get(key)
    if sessions is None:
        if DataClient is None:
            raise AcnDataNotConfigured('acnportal is not installed')
        cond = f'connectionTime >= "{format_datetime(start, usegmt=True)}" and connectionTime <= "{format_datetime(end, usegmt=True)}"'
        try:
            # ACNPortal 0.3.3 has no timeout argument. Scope a timeout-capable
            # transport to its module only; do not change global requests.get.
            with _client_lock, patch('acnportal.acndata.data_client.requests', SimpleNamespace(get=_get)):
                client = DataClient(credential)
                sessions = []
                started = time.monotonic()
                for session in client.get_sessions(site, cond=cond, timeseries=True):
                    if time.monotonic()-started > 300 or len(sessions) >= 5000:
                        raise AcnDataError('ACN query exceeded bounded demo fetch limits')
                    # Strip user identity and unneeded fields before storing or exporting.
                    cleaned = {k: session[k] for k in ('sessionID', 'connectionTime', 'disconnectTime',
                               'kWhDelivered', 'chargingCurrent', 'timezone', 'make', 'model', 'year') if k in session}
                    cleaned['userInputs'] = [{'kWhRequested': row['kWhRequested']} for row in
                                             (session.get('userInputs') or []) if 'kWhRequested' in row]
                    if start <= _datetime(cleaned['connectionTime']) < end:
                        sessions.append(cleaned)
            _cache_put(key, sessions)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise AcnDataError('ACN fetch failed ('+type(exc).__name__+')') from None
    result = []
    for session in sessions:
        try:
            energy = float(session['kWhDelivered'])
            if math.isfinite(energy) and energy >= min_kwh and max(_current_values(session), default=0) > 0:
                result.append(session)
        except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):
            continue
    return result


def recent_window(days=42, site='caltech'):
    """Most recent *available* complete site-local days, not the current calendar."""
    credential = _token()
    if not 1 <= days <= 62 or site not in ('caltech', 'jpl', 'office001'):
        raise ValueError('Invalid recent window')
    override = os.environ.get('ACN_DATA_END')
    if override and len(override) == 10:
        end = datetime.strptime(override, '%Y-%m-%d').replace(tzinfo=ZoneInfo('America/Los_Angeles'))
        return end-timedelta(days=days), end
    latest = {'connectionTime': override, 'timezone': 'America/Los_Angeles'} if override else _cache_get('latest:'+site)
    if latest is None:
        try:
            response = _get(API+'sessions/'+site, params={'sort': '-connectionTime', 'max_results': 1}, auth=(credential, ''))
            row = response.json()['_items'][0]
            latest = {'connectionTime': row['connectionTime'], 'timezone': row.get('timezone', 'America/Los_Angeles')}
            _cache_put('latest:'+site, latest)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise AcnDataError('Latest ACN date unavailable ('+type(exc).__name__+')') from None
    end = _local(latest, 'connectionTime').replace(hour=0, minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0)
    return end-timedelta(days=days), end


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def _ev_catalog():
    # The supplied website documents an API implementation, not a stable public
    # API hostname. Consume its documented public JSON release over HTTPS.
    release = _get('https://api.github.com/repos/open-ev-data/open-ev-data-dataset/releases/latest').json()
    assets = [a for a in release.get('assets', []) if a['name'].endswith('.json') and 'open-ev-data' in a['name']]
    if not assets:
        raise ValueError('No OpenEV JSON release found')
    payload = _get(assets[0]['browser_download_url']).json()
    return payload.get('vehicles', []) if isinstance(payload, dict) else payload


@lru_cache(maxsize=256)
def battery_capacity_for(make=None, model=None, year=None):
    if make and model and year:
        try:
            matches = [row for row in _ev_catalog() if
                       str(row.get('brand', row.get('make', ''))).casefold() == str(make).casefold()
                       and str(row.get('model', '')).casefold() == str(model).casefold()
                       and str(row.get('year', '')) == str(year)]
            values = {float(row['battery']['pack_capacity_kwh_net']) for row in matches
                      if row.get('battery', {}).get('pack_capacity_kwh_net')}
            if len(values) == 1 and 0 < next(iter(values)) <= 250:
                return values.pop()
        except Exception:
            pass
    LOG.warning('Battery capacity unknown or lookup unavailable; using assumed 40 kWh')
    return 40.0


def simulate_soc_trace(session, capacity_kwh=None, initial_soc=0.3, voltage=208, efficiency=0.9):
    capacity = float(capacity_kwh if capacity_kwh is not None else battery_capacity_for(
        session.get('make'), session.get('model'), session.get('year')))
    energy = float(session['kWhDelivered'])
    if not all(math.isfinite(v) for v in (capacity, energy, initial_soc, voltage, efficiency)) or capacity <= 0 or energy < 0 or voltage <= 0 or not 0 < efficiency <= 1 or not 0 <= initial_soc <= 1:
        raise ValueError('Invalid SoC model inputs')
    arrival, departure = _local(session, 'connectionTime'), _local(session, 'disconnectTime')
    duration = (departure.astimezone(timezone.utc)-arrival.astimezone(timezone.utc)).total_seconds()/3600
    if duration <= 0:
        raise ValueError('Invalid session duration')
    series = session.get('chargingCurrent') or {}
    currents = _current_values(session)
    if not currents or max(currents) <= 0:
        raise ValueError('Missing positive current samples')
    timestamps = series.get('timestamps', []) if isinstance(series, dict) else []
    if timestamps:
        if len(timestamps) != len(currents):
            raise ValueError('Current and timestamp lengths differ')
        times = [_datetime(t).astimezone(timezone.utc) for t in timestamps]
        if any(b <= a for a, b in zip(times, times[1:])):
            raise ValueError('Current timestamps must increase')
        # ACN includes logger padding before connection and after disconnect.
        # Integrate only measured samples inside the actual connected interval.
        pairs = [(t, c) for t, c in zip(times, currents)
                 if arrival.astimezone(timezone.utc) <= t <= departure.astimezone(timezone.utc)]
        if len(pairs) < 2 or max(c for _, c in pairs) <= 0:
            raise ValueError('Insufficient current samples inside the session')
        times, currents = map(list, zip(*pairs))
    else:
        # Numeric fixture/legacy traces have an explicitly inferred sample period.
        times = [arrival.astimezone(timezone.utc)+timedelta(hours=duration*i/max(1, len(currents)-1)) for i in range(len(currents))]
    peak = max(currents)
    tail = currents[-max(1, math.ceil(len(currents)*.2)):]
    taper = sum(tail)/len(tail) < .5*peak
    estimate, assumption = initial_soc, 'supplied/default initial SoC'
    requested = next((r.get('kWhRequested') for r in reversed(session.get('userInputs') or [])
                      if r.get('kWhRequested') is not None), None)
    if requested is not None and math.isfinite(float(requested)) and float(requested) >= 0:
        estimate = min(.95, max(.05, 1-float(requested)/capacity))
        assumption = 'inferred from requested energy and assumed full target'
    elif taper:
        estimate = min(.95, max(.05, .95-energy/capacity))
        assumption = 'inferred from observed taper and assumed 95% final SoC'
    final = min(1.0, estimate+energy/capacity)
    weights = [0.0]
    cumulative = 0.0
    for i in range(1, len(currents)):
        dt = (times[i]-times[i-1]).total_seconds()/3600
        provisional = estimate+cumulative/capacity
        # Preserve already-observed taper; otherwise apply a simple CV shape.
        factor = 1.0 if taper or provisional <= .8 else max(.05, (1-provisional)/.2)
        weight = .5*(currents[i-1]+currents[i])*voltage/1000*efficiency*dt*factor
        cumulative += weight
        weights.append(weight)
    total = sum(weights)
    if len(currents) < 2 or total <= 0:
        raise ValueError('Insufficient current integration interval')
    accumulated = 0.0
    points = []
    for timestamp, weight in zip(times, weights):
        accumulated += weight
        points.append({'t': timestamp.isoformat(), 'soc': min(final, estimate+(final-estimate)*accumulated/total)})
    points[-1]['soc'] = final
    return {'soc_series': points, 'initial_soc_estimate': estimate, 'initial_soc_assumption': assumption,
            'final_soc': final, 'capacity_kwh': capacity, 'max_kw_observed': peak*voltage/1000,
            'taper_observed': taper, 'arrival_hour': arrival.hour+arrival.minute/60+arrival.second/3600,
            'departure_hour': departure.hour+departure.minute/60+departure.second/3600,
            'duration_hours': duration, 'soc_source': 'model-derived, not measured',
            'unrepresented_energy_kwh': max(0.0, energy-(final-estimate)*capacity),
            'energy_convention': 'Current/efficiency determine shape; normalized to recorded kWhDelivered; SoC capped at 1'}


def to_demand_samples(sessions, dataset='acn-caltech-v1'):
    if not sessions:
        return []
    earliest = min(_local(s, 'connectionTime').date() for s in sessions)
    bins = {}
    for session in sessions:
        local = _local(session, 'connectionTime')
        energy = float(session['kWhDelivered'])
        if not math.isfinite(energy) or energy < 0:
            raise ValueError('Invalid delivered energy')
        key = ((local.date()-earliest).days, local.hour)
        bins[key] = bins.get(key, 0)+energy
    return [{'dataset': dataset, 'day': d, 'hour': h, 'energy_kwh': value,
             'source': 'acn-data-caltech'} for (d, h), value in sorted(bins.items())]


def to_vehicle_sessions(sessions, capacity_lookup=battery_capacity_for):
    from sender.station_core import Vehicle
    if not sessions:
        return []
    # Replay one coherent latest day, rather than folding unrelated days together.
    latest = max(_local(s, 'connectionTime').date() for s in sessions)
    fleet, ids = [], set()
    for session in sorted(sessions, key=lambda s: _datetime(s['connectionTime'])):
        if _local(session, 'connectionTime').date() != latest:
            continue
        try:
            capacity = capacity_lookup(session.get('make'), session.get('model'), session.get('year'))
            trace = simulate_soc_trace(session, capacity)
            identifier = str(session['sessionID'])[:8]
            if identifier in ids:
                identifier = hashlib.sha256(str(session['sessionID']).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
            target = min(trace['initial_soc_estimate']+float(session['kWhDelivered'])/capacity+.05, .95)
            if target <= trace['initial_soc_estimate']:
                continue
            vehicle = Vehicle(identifier, trace['arrival_hour'], trace['arrival_hour']+trace['duration_hours'],
                              capacity, trace['initial_soc_estimate'], target, min(trace['max_kw_observed'], 7.0))
            vehicle.soc_trace = trace  # In memory only; not added to the SQLite schema or MQTT payload.
            vehicle.data_source = 'acn-data-caltech'
            fleet.append(vehicle)
            ids.add(identifier)
            if len(fleet) == 6:
                break
        except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):
            continue
    return fleet


if __name__ == '__main__':
    import argparse
    from dataclasses import asdict
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Download resumable ACN sessions into data/acn_downloads')
    parser.add_argument('--download', action='store_true', required=True)
    parser.add_argument('--days', type=int, default=42)
    parser.add_argument('--seconds', type=int, default=120)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.seconds <= 0:
        parser.error('--seconds must be positive')
    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.WARNING, format='%(levelname)s %(message)s')
    sessions = download_local(args.days, args.seconds)
    fleet = to_vehicle_sessions(sessions)
    if fleet:
        _write_json(Path(__file__).with_name('sender')/'acn_vehicles.json',
                    {'source': 'acn-data-caltech', 'vehicles': [asdict(v) for v in fleet]})
        print('Prepared sender/acn_vehicles.json with', len(fleet), 'vehicles')

# Two laptops through the public EMQX broker

## Ready-to-run configuration

The project is configured for **broker.emqx.io:8883**, using verified TLS and no
username/password. Only the broker is hosted on the internet; the project laptop
still runs Node-RED, SQLite, ML and the dashboard.

```text
Sensor laptop -> public EMQX broker -> project laptop
Sensor laptop <- public EMQX broker <- control commands
```

The shared `mqtt-public.json` selects these topics:

- `gridsense/team3/b7749f4efd894610/telemetry`
- `gridsense/team3/b7749f4efd894610/actuator`
- `gridsense/team3/b7749f4efd894610/ack`

Both laptops must have the same copy of that file. Unique topics reduce collisions;
they are not private or authenticated. Other public-broker users can read messages
or publish to these topics. Do not publish personal data; the ACN adapter sends only aggregate/derived demo data. TLS verifies the server and
encrypts transport; it does not restrict other clients' topic access. See the
[official public broker information](https://www.emqx.com/en/mqtt/public-mqtt5-broker).

## Project laptop (Windows)

Install dependencies using README if not already installed. Stop the previous
launcher, then run from the project folder:

```powershell
./start.ps1 -PublicBroker
```

This automatically generates `node-red/flows.public.json` with TLS, the broker
address, a unique client ID and the shared topics, then starts Node-RED and the
Python database/decision service. No local broker or sensor is started. No manual
Node-RED broker editing, cloud account or MQTT credentials are needed.

Open `http://127.0.0.1:1880/dashboard/station` for the dashboard and
`http://127.0.0.1:1880` for the editor. Keep this launcher running.

## Sensor laptop

Copy these **five current files** into one folder:

1. `simulator.py`
2. `actuator.py`
3. `station_core.py`
4. `tariff.py`
5. `mqtt-public.json`

Install Python 3.10+ and run in that folder:

```bash
python -m pip install "paho-mqtt>=2.1,<3"
python simulator.py --public-broker
```

Use `python3` if required by your OS. Add `--tou-demo` for the evening tariff demo.
The simulator prints the broker and topics when connected. Public mode does not
send private MQTT credentials even if MQTT_USERNAME/MQTT_PASSWORD are set.

Both laptops need internet access; they do not need the same Wi-Fi. No inbound
MQTT port or router port forwarding is required. Keep both clocks synchronized
for meaningful response-time measurements. Only run one live simulator for this
station. Stop the simulator and project launcher with Ctrl+C when finished.

## Verify the demonstration

1. The Node-RED MQTT nodes should show connected status.
2. Dashboard readings should update from the other laptop.
3. Accepted messages in the acknowledgement debug output confirm that decisions
   returned through MQTT and were applied by the sensor laptop.
4. Try the sensor laptop on another internet connection to demonstrate the split.

The project has already passed a real-broker integration test using local test
clients connected through `broker.emqx.io`, not a local broker. It verified five
accepted commands, persisted telemetry/acknowledgements and trained decisions.
See `artifacts/public_mqtt_check.json`. This is not a test of your two physical
laptops. Reproduce with `python tests/public_mqtt_check.py` after stopping the demo;
this uses separate temporary test topics and writes test cycles to `station.db`.

## History and model training

With the public-mode project launcher running, use this command on either laptop:

```bash
python simulator.py --public-broker --history
```

On the project laptop, wait until `http://127.0.0.1:8765/health` reports 1,008 history
rows, then run `python ml_train.py` using the project environment. Restart the
project launcher to load refreshed models. Run history replay from the project root to enable ACN fetching. It uses the
same public broker and explicitly labels real versus fallback data. Demo setup
trains local models; it does not ship cross-version model binaries.

## Other modes and troubleshooting

- `./start.ps1` keeps the original single-laptop local demo.
- `./start.ps1 -RemoteSensor` uses a local LAN broker as described in README.
- `./start.ps1 -CloudBroker` is for a private hosted broker configured manually in
  the original Node-RED flow; it does not select the public profile.
- Generated public-flow edits are replaced at startup. Make shared application
  changes in `node-red/flows.json` or the public-flow generator.
- For Linux/macOS project hosts, run `node node-red/build-public-flow.cjs`, start
  `python station_service.py`, and in another terminal run
  `node node-red/node_modules/node-red/red.js --userDir node-red --settings node-red/settings.js node-red/flows.public.json`.
- If connection fails, check internet access and outbound TCP 8883. The public broker
  is a shared test service; it may be unavailable. Certificate failures should be
  fixed by checking the clock/trust store, never by disabling verification.
- A private broker can use `python simulator.py --broker-host HOST --tls --username USER`;
  the password is prompted. Configure matching credentials, TLS and topics in Node-RED.

If another team uses this submission independently, choose a different unique
`topic_prefix` in `mqtt-public.json`, copy it to both laptops and restart both sides.

## Real data source (ACN-Data)

ACN-Data is the preferred source for historical charging behavior and replay fleets.
Register and retrieve an API token at [Caltech ACN-Data](https://ev.caltech.edu/dataset).
On the **project laptop only**, set `ACN_DATA_TOKEN` in the current terminal (enter
its value locally, never commit it or paste it into chat):

```powershell
$env:ACN_DATA_TOKEN = '<your API token>'
# With the project services already running:
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe simulator.py --public-broker --history
Invoke-RestMethod http://127.0.0.1:8765/health
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe ml_train.py
```

Restart the project after training. `/health` exposes `data_source`, `dataset`,
`history_rows` and `active_history_rows`. The model report and dashboard also show
`data_source`. `ml_train.py --dataset acn-caltech-v1` explicitly selects real data;
otherwise training selects the most recently ingested dataset without mixing sources.

The adapter uses `acnportal.acndata.DataClient(token).get_sessions(...)` from the
published 0.3.3 release (`acnportal>=0.3.3,<0.4`). Session queries use bounded date
windows and time series. It discovers the latest available date rather than assuming
that data extends to today; optionally set `ACN_DATA_END=2020-02-12` to select a
specific exclusive end date (site-local midnight). The default site is Caltech;
`acn_data.fetch_sessions` also accepts `jpl` and `office001` for explicit use.

Fetched sessions are cached locally in `artifacts/acn_cache.json` for 24 hours,
keyed by site/start/end. Calls have HTTP timeouts and a bounded 300-second/5,000-session
fetch budget; incomplete downloads are rejected instead of reported as complete data.
The most recent incomplete site-local day is excluded. At least 18 days with observed
sessions are required; missing arrival hours within the fetched interval are zeros.
Each session's total delivered energy is assigned to its arrival-hour bin: this is
arrival-associated energy demand, **not** the measured power consumed in that hour.

If the token is missing, access fails, or data is insufficient, `[FALLBACK] Using
synthetic history` is logged. The unchanged deterministic generator is used and
reported as `synthetic-fallback`; prediction validity is limited to the simulation.
The service logs the fallback when that history reaches SQLite. To force offline data:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe simulator.py --public-broker --history --synthetic-only
```

A successful project-laptop history replay also prepares `sender/acn_vehicles.json`.
Copy that file alongside the five sender files to **your sensor laptop**, then run
`python simulator.py --public-broker`. This file contains at most six derived vehicle
records from the latest session day, not raw current traces or credentials. The sensor
still installs **only paho-mqtt**. ACNPortal and requests remain project-laptop
requirements. If no prepared fleet is available, the standalone sensor explicitly
falls back. `--synthetic-only` bypasses even the prepared fleet. `--tou-demo` remains
the deliberately synthetic tariff scenario.

ACN-Data normally does not contain measured SoC or a vehicle make/model/year.
SoC is model-derived from native current timestamps, recorded session energy and
an assumed/known capacity. OpenEV's documented API requires hosting; the adapter
uses the project's public JSON release (discovered through its GitHub releases API)
when vehicle identity is supplied. Unknown/unavailable/ambiguous capacity uses a
logged **40 kWh assumption**, not an assertion about the actual car. Lookups are
cached in memory. See [OpenEV data consumption](https://open-ev-data.github.io/latest/dataset/HOW_TO_CONSUME/).

Current, voltage and efficiency determine trajectory shape; the trace is normalized
to `kWhDelivered`, so the energy-to-SoC convention matches the requested model.
Requested energy is preferred for initial-SoC inference; observed taper permits an
explicit 95%-final-SoC assumption, otherwise the supplied/default 30% initial SoC is
used. SoC is bounded to [0,1], with excess unrepresentable energy reported separately.
This is a calibrated simulation, not measured battery energy or a BMS model. Current
power and real SoC are not identified from session energy alone. Overnight departures
retain their duration; live actuation uses the existing piecewise taper model.

Tokens are read from the environment (or an explicit in-memory API argument), never
logged or written to cache. The submission ZIP excludes `.env*`, downloaded sessions,
ACN caches, and prepared fleets. Only aggregate energy/derived fleet data crosses
MQTT; do not add personal data to public broker messages.

## Saved ACN downloads and offline reuse

Download incrementally on the project laptop after setting ACN_DATA_TOKEN:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe acn_data.py --download --seconds 120
```

Sessions are saved after every response in `data/acn_downloads/caltech-YYYY-MM-DD.json`.
Run the same command again to resume incomplete pages and skip completed days. The
seconds limit is checked between requests; an in-flight request can take additional
time. Each file records whether the whole day was downloaded. Credentials and user
identity fields are excluded. These durable historical snapshots do not expire like
the separate 24-hour API cache; rerunning the downloader extends the saved snapshot.

The root simulator automatically prefers this folder, with no token or network
needed to read it. Live mode can use valid sessions from a partial day. History mode
requires at least 18 observed days within a contiguous, completely downloaded
interval. Partial or missing days are never converted to zero-demand training rows.
Insufficient saved history logs a warning and uses synthetic fallback immediately.
A live ACN fleet therefore does not imply the ML training data is also ACN; check the
model report and /health separately. `--synthetic-only` bypasses saved data too.

The downloader also writes `sender/acn_vehicles.json` when usable vehicles exist.
Include that file when manually sending the sender folder. The standalone sender
can replay it with only paho-mqtt installed. Raw downloaded sessions remain on the
project laptop and are excluded from Git and the submission ZIP. The ZIP includes
the downloader code; download there or copy the data folder separately if needed.

FILE: decision_engine.py

"""Performance-gated forecast plus dwell time and power deadband."""
import json
import math
import pickle
import time
from pathlib import Path
from sender.station_core import schedule, site_limit_kw, edf_key
from tou_scheduler import apply_tou
from station_store import DB, database, read_state

_cache = {}


def load_bundle(path):
    path = Path(path).resolve()
    key = str(path)
    if key not in _cache:
        # Only load locally trained, trusted artifacts; pickle is not a data interchange format.
        bundle = pickle.loads(path.read_bytes()) if path.exists() else None
        if bundle:
            from ml_train import predict
            predict(bundle, 0)  # Warm imports, transforms and model before serving traffic.
        _cache[key] = bundle
    return _cache[key]


def initialize_models():
    """Call before binding HTTP port. New artifacts require a service restart."""
    return load_bundle(Path(__file__).with_name('artifacts')/'bundle.pkl')


def stabilize(state, plan, previous):
    """Hold valid occupants for >=6 simulated minutes; safety events may override.

    Preserve a socket for a continuing EV even after dwell expires. Rates within
    0.25 kW of the previous plan are held unless capacity/target changes demand it.
    """
    previous = previous or {}
    now = state['hour']
    active = {e['id']:e for e in state['vehicles'] if e['arrival'] <= now < e['departure'] and e['remaining_kwh'] > 1e-8}
    previous_assignments = {a['ev_id']:a for a in previous.get('assignments',[])}
    since = previous.get('assigned_since',{})
    chosen = []
    for a in previous.get('assignments',[]):
        if a['ev_id'] in active and now-since.get(a['ev_id'],now) < .1-1e-9:
            chosen.append(a['ev_id'])
    for a in plan['assignments']:
        if a['ev_id'] not in chosen and len(chosen) < len(state['chargers']):
            chosen.append(a['ev_id'])
    chosen.sort(key=lambda eid: edf_key(active[eid], now))
    # Existing assignments keep their physical socket; fill vacancies afterwards.
    sockets = {eid:previous_assignments[eid]['charger_id'] for eid in chosen if eid in previous_assignments}
    free = [c for c in state['chargers'] if c not in sockets.values()]
    for eid in chosen:
        if eid not in sockets:
            sockets[eid] = free.pop(0)
    remaining = max(0,site_limit_kw(state)-state['base_kw'])
    assignments = []
    for eid in chosen:
        ev = active[eid]
        desired = min(ev['max_kw'], remaining)
        old = previous_assignments.get(eid,{}).get('rate_kw')
        if old is not None and abs(desired-old) < .25:
            desired = old
        rate = math.floor(max(0,min(desired,ev['max_kw'],remaining))*100)/100
        remaining -= rate
        assignments.append({'ev_id':eid,'charger_id':sockets[eid],'rate_kw':rate})
    # No redistribution after deadband: that used to undo held power rates.
    plan['assignments'] = assignments
    plan['assigned_since'] = {eid:since.get(eid,now) if eid in previous_assignments else now for eid in chosen}
    plan['stability_policy'] = {'minimum_dwell_sim_minutes':6,'rate_deadband_kw':.25,
                               'override':'completion, departure and electrical safety'}
    return plan


def decide(key, path=DB, bundle_path=None, tou_enabled=True):
    started = time.perf_counter()
    state = read_state(key,path)  # No runtime sensor stream is used as a training input.
    bundle_path = bundle_path or Path(__file__).with_name('artifacts')/'bundle.pkl'
    bundle = load_bundle(bundle_path)
    if bundle:
        from ml_train import predict
        forecast, latency = predict(bundle,state['hour'])
        baseline = bundle['baseline']
        evaluation = bundle['report']
        selection = evaluation['selected_model']
    else:
        forecast, baseline, latency = 0,0,0
        evaluation = {'models':{},'selected_model':'untrained_safe_rules'}
        selection = 'untrained_safe_rules'
    # Existing scheduler reads a profile; replace ONLY the next-hour forecast.
    profile = [baseline]*24
    profile[int(state['hour']+1)%24] = forecast
    plan = schedule(state,{'profile_kwh':profile})
    plan.update(model=selection,performance=evaluation,inference_ms=latency,
                forecast_next_hour_kwh=forecast,run_id=state['run_id'],sensor_timestamp=state.get('timestamp',0))
    if not bundle:
        plan['alerts'].append('Models not trained: using safe deadline rules. Replay history and run ml_train.py.')
    with database(path) as conn:
        row = conn.execute('SELECT decision FROM cycles WHERE run_id=? AND sequence<? AND decision IS NOT NULL ORDER BY sequence DESC LIMIT 1',
                           (key['run_id'],key['sequence'])).fetchone()
        previous = json.loads(row['decision']) if row else None
    plan = apply_tou(state, plan, previous, enabled=tou_enabled)
    # The legacy stabilizer reallocates full available power, undoing price deferral.
    # Use it only for the fallback policy; certified ToU rates must remain intact.
    if plan['tou_status'] in ('disabled', 'fallback_edf'):
        plan = stabilize(state,plan,previous)
    plan['decision_ms'] = (time.perf_counter()-started)*1000
    with database(path) as conn:
        conn.execute('UPDATE cycles SET decision=? WHERE run_id=? AND sequence=?',(json.dumps(plan),key['run_id'],key['sequence']))
    return {'state':state,'decision':plan}

# GridSense - run the demonstration

This package contains only the files used to run the application and sensor demo.
The project laptop runs Node-RED, SQLite, ML and the dashboard. The sensor laptop
publishes simulated readings through `broker.emqx.io:8883` using verified TLS.

## 1. Set up sir's Windows laptop (once)

Install Python 3.10 or newer (Python 3.11 is supported) and Node.js 20 or later,
with both available on PATH. Setup installs compatible versions using
`requirements-station.txt`. Installing dependencies requires internet access.
The ZIP does not include Python, Node.js, a virtual environment or node_modules.

Extract the ZIP, open PowerShell in its `GridSense` folder, then run:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\setup_demo.ps1
```

This creates a local Python environment, installs compatible dependencies and
trains five demo models for that environment, preferring ACN data when configured. It does not modify your permanent
execution policy. Training may take a few minutes on a slower laptop.

Model preparation is an installation step: real-or-fallback history is staged
in a temporary SQLite database, then the models are trained from those records.
Only the resulting runtime bundle is kept. The live demo still uses the full
MQTT -> Node-RED -> SQLite -> ML/decision -> MQTT flow.

## 2. Start the project on sir's laptop

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\start_demo.ps1
```

Open **http://127.0.0.1:1880/dashboard/station** in the browser. Keep the PowerShell
window open. Do not run another copy of Node-RED or the project on ports 1880/8765.

## 3. Start the sensor

Copy the entire `sender` folder to the sensor laptop. It contains five runtime files, plus an optional prepared ACN fleet;
do not remove `station_core.py` or `tariff.py` from the project laptop because
the decision engine also imports them.

Open a terminal inside the copied `sender` folder and run:

```bash
python -m pip install "paho-mqtt>=2.1,<3"
python simulator.py --public-broker --tou-demo
```

The evening scenario demonstrates tariff-aware charging. Omit `--tou-demo` for
the default congested scenario. Both laptops need internet, but may use different
Wi-Fi networks. Keep the same `mqtt-public.json` on both sides. No account, MQTT
password, manual Node-RED configuration or router port forwarding is required.

For a one-laptop demonstration, keep `start_demo.ps1` running and open a second
PowerShell window in `GridSense`:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe sender\simulator.py --public-broker --tou-demo
```

Even in this mode, messages travel through the internet MQTT broker.

## 4. Check the result and stop

The dashboard shows incoming readings, model comparison and charging decisions.
Open http://127.0.0.1:1880 for the Node-RED editor; accepted acknowledgement
messages in its debug sidebar confirm the return control path. Press Ctrl+C in
both the project and sensor terminals when finished.

The live SQLite database is created automatically on first use. Setup creates
`artifacts/bundle.pkl` with the selected trained model and all five model-comparison
results. `artifacts/runtime.json` records the local dependency versions. No model
trained under another Python environment is shipped in the ZIP. To force fully
offline preparation, run `setup_demo.ps1 -SyntheticOnly`.

## Retraining

If you change Python or ML package versions, stop the project and rerun
`setup_demo.ps1` to rebuild a compatible bundle. For an explicit demonstration
of history ingestion through the broker, keep the project running.
In a second project terminal:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe sender\simulator.py --public-broker --history
Invoke-RestMethod http://127.0.0.1:8765/health
# Wait until history_rows is 1008, then:
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe ml_train.py
```

Restart `start_demo.ps1` after training. The history replay remains synthetic and
follows MQTT -> Node-RED -> SQLite before training reads the database.

## Troubleshooting and limits

- No readings: check both internet connections, sender output and outbound TCP 8883.
- Service exits: read `service.err.log` and `nodered.err.log` created in this folder.
- TLS errors: check system clock and certificate trust; do not disable verification.
- Use only one live sensor for the shared topic prefix. Synchronize laptop clocks
  for meaningful response-time measurements.
- The public broker is shared and may be unavailable. Topics are not private;
  only simulated data should be sent. TLS does not prevent others publishing to
  public topics. The demo controls simulated actuators, not physical equipment.

Tests, reports, slides, logs, backups, databases and development utilities are
intentionally omitted from this demo package.

## Real data source (ACN-Data)

ACN-Data is the preferred source for historical charging behavior and replay fleets.
Register and retrieve an API token at [Caltech ACN-Data](https://ev.caltech.edu/dataset).
On the **project laptop only**, set `ACN_DATA_TOKEN` in the current terminal (enter
its value locally, never commit it or paste it into chat):

```powershell
$env:ACN_DATA_TOKEN = '<your API token>'
# With the project services already running:
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe simulator.py --public-broker --history
Invoke-RestMethod http://127.0.0.1:8765/health
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe ml_train.py
```

Restart the project after training. `/health` exposes `data_source`, `dataset`,
`history_rows` and `active_history_rows`. The model report and dashboard also show
`data_source`. `ml_train.py --dataset acn-caltech-v1` explicitly selects real data;
otherwise training selects the most recently ingested dataset without mixing sources.

The adapter uses `acnportal.acndata.DataClient(token).get_sessions(...)` from the
published 0.3.3 release (`acnportal>=0.3.3,<0.4`). Session queries use bounded date
windows and time series. It discovers the latest available date rather than assuming
that data extends to today; optionally set `ACN_DATA_END=2020-02-12` to select a
specific exclusive end date (site-local midnight). The default site is Caltech;
`acn_data.fetch_sessions` also accepts `jpl` and `office001` for explicit use.

Fetched sessions are cached locally in `artifacts/acn_cache.json` for 24 hours,
keyed by site/start/end. Calls have HTTP timeouts and a bounded 300-second/5,000-session
fetch budget; incomplete downloads are rejected instead of reported as complete data.
The most recent incomplete site-local day is excluded. At least 18 days with observed
sessions are required; missing arrival hours within the fetched interval are zeros.
Each session's total delivered energy is assigned to its arrival-hour bin: this is
arrival-associated energy demand, **not** the measured power consumed in that hour.

If the token is missing, access fails, or data is insufficient, `[FALLBACK] Using
synthetic history` is logged. The unchanged deterministic generator is used and
reported as `synthetic-fallback`; prediction validity is limited to the simulation.
The service logs the fallback when that history reaches SQLite. To force offline data:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe simulator.py --public-broker --history --synthetic-only
```

A successful project-laptop history replay also prepares `sender/acn_vehicles.json`.
Copy that file alongside the five sender files to **your sensor laptop**, then run
`python simulator.py --public-broker`. This file contains at most six derived vehicle
records from the latest session day, not raw current traces or credentials. The sensor
still installs **only paho-mqtt**. ACNPortal and requests remain project-laptop
requirements. If no prepared fleet is available, the standalone sensor explicitly
falls back. `--synthetic-only` bypasses even the prepared fleet. `--tou-demo` remains
the deliberately synthetic tariff scenario.

ACN-Data normally does not contain measured SoC or a vehicle make/model/year.
SoC is model-derived from native current timestamps, recorded session energy and
an assumed/known capacity. OpenEV's documented API requires hosting; the adapter
uses the project's public JSON release (discovered through its GitHub releases API)
when vehicle identity is supplied. Unknown/unavailable/ambiguous capacity uses a
logged **40 kWh assumption**, not an assertion about the actual car. Lookups are
cached in memory. See [OpenEV data consumption](https://open-ev-data.github.io/latest/dataset/HOW_TO_CONSUME/).

Current, voltage and efficiency determine trajectory shape; the trace is normalized
to `kWhDelivered`, so the energy-to-SoC convention matches the requested model.
Requested energy is preferred for initial-SoC inference; observed taper permits an
explicit 95%-final-SoC assumption, otherwise the supplied/default 30% initial SoC is
used. SoC is bounded to [0,1], with excess unrepresentable energy reported separately.
This is a calibrated simulation, not measured battery energy or a BMS model. Current
power and real SoC are not identified from session energy alone. Overnight departures
retain their duration; live actuation uses the existing piecewise taper model.

Tokens are read from the environment (or an explicit in-memory API argument), never
logged or written to cache. The submission ZIP excludes `.env*`, downloaded sessions,
ACN caches, and prepared fleets. Only aggregate energy/derived fleet data crosses
MQTT; do not add personal data to public broker messages.

## Saved ACN downloads and offline reuse

Download incrementally on the project laptop after setting ACN_DATA_TOKEN:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe acn_data.py --download --seconds 120
```

Sessions are saved after every response in `data/acn_downloads/caltech-YYYY-MM-DD.json`.
Run the same command again to resume incomplete pages and skip completed days. The
seconds limit is checked between requests; an in-flight request can take additional
time. Each file records whether the whole day was downloaded. Credentials and user
identity fields are excluded. These durable historical snapshots do not expire like
the separate 24-hour API cache; rerunning the downloader extends the saved snapshot.

The root simulator automatically prefers this folder, with no token or network
needed to read it. Live mode can use valid sessions from a partial day. History mode
requires at least 18 observed days within a contiguous, completely downloaded
interval. Partial or missing days are never converted to zero-demand training rows.
Insufficient saved history logs a warning and uses synthetic fallback immediately.
A live ACN fleet therefore does not imply the ML training data is also ACN; check the
model report and /health separately. `--synthetic-only` bypasses saved data too.

The downloader also writes `sender/acn_vehicles.json` when usable vehicles exist.
Include that file when manually sending the sender folder. The standalone sender
can replay it with only paho-mqtt installed. Raw downloaded sessions remain on the
project laptop and are excluded from Git and the submission ZIP. The ZIP includes
the downloader code; download there or copy the data folder separately if needed.

FILE: evaluate_control.py
================================================
"""Repeatable full-scenario closed-loop comparison (simulated, not network timing)."""
import json
import pickle
import time
from pathlib import Path
from sender.station_core import Station, schedule
from decision_engine import stabilize
from ml_train import ARTIFACTS, predict


def evaluate(feasible=False):
    bundle=pickle.loads((ARTIFACTS/'bundle.pkl').read_bytes())
    results={}
    for name in ['constant_baseline',*bundle['report']['models']]:
        model=None if name=='constant_baseline' else pickle.loads((ARTIFACTS/f'{name}.pkl').read_bytes())
        current={**bundle,'model':model}
        station=Station()
        if feasible:
            for ev in station.vehicles:
                ev.target = min(ev.target, ev.soc+.05)
        previous=None
        switches=rapid=violations=0
        last_mapping={}
        last_switch={}
        peak=0
        latencies=[]
        for _ in range(185):
            state=station.snapshot()
            start=time.perf_counter()
            forecast,_=predict(current,state['hour'])
            profile=[bundle['baseline']]*24
            profile[int(state['hour']+1)%24]=forecast
            plan=stabilize(state,schedule(state,{'profile_kwh':profile}),previous)
            assert station.accept(plan)
            latencies.append((time.perf_counter()-start)*1000)
            mapping={a['charger_id']:a['ev_id'] for a in plan['assignments'] if a['rate_kw']>0}
            for charger in station.chargers:
                if last_mapping.get(charger)!=mapping.get(charger):
                    switches+=1
                    if charger in last_switch and station.hour-last_switch[charger]<.1-1e-8:
                        rapid+=1
                    last_switch[charger]=station.hour
            last_mapping=mapping
            previous=plan
            station.step(1/30)
            observed=station.snapshot()
            peak=max(peak,observed['load_kw'])
            violations+=int(observed['load_kw']>observed['capacity_kw']+1e-6)
        required=sum(e.delivered_kwh+e.remaining for e in station.vehicles)
        results[name]={'deadline_success_fraction':sum(e.remaining<=.01 for e in station.vehicles)/len(station.vehicles),
                       'energy_fulfillment_fraction':sum(e.delivered_kwh for e in station.vehicles)/required,
                       'unmet_energy_kwh':sum(e.remaining for e in station.vehicles),
                       'peak_site_kw':peak,'capacity_violation_samples':violations,
                       'socket_switches':switches,'rapid_switches':rapid,
                       'mean_local_decision_ms':sum(latencies)/len(latencies)}
    report={'scenario':'feasible low-energy requests' if feasible else 'congested original requests',
            'definition':'Same six-EV scenario and initial state for each model; 185 steps; timing excludes MQTT/Node-RED. Full network timing is in live_check.json.',
            'results':results}
    filename = 'control_feasible.json' if feasible else 'control_comparison.json'
    (ARTIFACTS/filename).write_text(json.dumps(report,indent=2))
    return report


if __name__=='__main__':
    print(json.dumps(evaluate(),indent=2))
    print(json.dumps(evaluate(feasible=True),indent=2))

FILE: evaluate_tou.py
================================================
"""Paired deterministic tariff demo; no MQTT publications or production DB writes."""
import json
from pathlib import Path
from sender.station_core import schedule, tou_demo_station
from tou_scheduler import apply_tou, CONTROL_HOURS
from sender.tariff import price_at


def evaluate():
    results = {}
    for policy in ('unmanaged', 'edf', 'tou'):
        station = tou_demo_station(unmanaged=policy == 'unmanaged')
        previous = None
        cost = energy = peak = 0.
        fallback = 0
        samples = []
        for step in range(240):
            station.hour = 16 + step * CONTROL_HOURS  # Avoid accumulated clock roundoff.
            state = station.snapshot()
            plan = schedule(state, {'profile_kwh': [0.] * 24})
            if policy == 'tou':
                plan = apply_tou(state, plan, previous)
                fallback += plan['tou_status'] == 'fallback_edf'
            if not station.accept(plan):
                raise AssertionError('Actuator rejected demo plan')
            before = sum(e.delivered_kwh for e in station.vehicles)
            station.step(CONTROL_HOURS)
            delivered = sum(e.delivered_kwh for e in station.vehicles) - before
            price = price_at(state['hour'])
            cost += delivered * price
            energy += delivered
            load = station.base_kw + delivered / CONTROL_HOURS
            peak = max(peak, load)
            samples.append({'hour': state['hour'], 'grid_kw': load, 'price_per_kwh': price})
            previous = plan
        results[policy] = dict(charging_cost_inr=round(cost, 4), delivered_kwh=round(energy, 6),
            peak_grid_kw=round(peak, 4), unmet_kwh=round(sum(e.remaining for e in station.vehicles), 6),
            deadlines_met=all(e.remaining <= .01 for e in station.vehicles),
            fallback_cycles=fallback, samples=samples)
    return {'scenario': 'Identical 4 kWh urgent and 12 kWh flexible requests, 16:00â€“24:00',
            'reference': 'Unmanaged removes the site limit for a counterfactual demand trace; same EVs, sockets, taper and deadlines.',
            'billing': 'EV energy only; fixed base load excluded from all three policies.',
            'results': results}


if __name__ == '__main__':
    report = evaluate()
    target = Path(__file__).with_name('artifacts') / 'tou_comparison.json'
    target.parent.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    target.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
    print(json.dumps({k: {m: v for m, v in r.items() if m != 'samples'}
                      for k, r in report['results'].items()}, indent=2))
